Now I understand the situation, and it's important. The merge base is #484. Since then:

- **`origin/main`** independently merged phase 42 (unpin guard #485) and phase 43 (fuse write journal #487) via their own PR branches.
- **This branch** contains its *own* sequential implementation of phases 42, 43, **and** 44.

So phases 42/43 exist on **both sides, implemented divergently** — that's what's conflicting. The phase 44 work is unique to this branch. This means many conflicts are "same feature, two versions" where I must judge whether main's reviewed version or this branch's version (possibly with later fixes on top) is canonical. Security-sensitive (unpin) code is involved, so I won't blind-resolve.

diff --git a/dev/fd/11 b/dev/fd/12
--- a/dev/fd/11
+++ b/dev/fd/12
@@ -239,29 +239,33 @@ export class VaultService {
    * Security properties (D-01..D-05):
    * - D-01: Ownership check; no-row path touches nothing (no oracle)
    * - D-02: Cross-user attempt increments unpinCrossUserAttempts metric + warns
+   *         (skipped when options.suppressCrossUserAudit is set — used by internal
+   *         upload-rollback compensation, which is not an external probe)
    * - D-03: Row delete IS the quota decrement (in-transaction); Kubo outside
    * - D-04: pg_advisory_xact_lock as first statement serializes concurrent deletes
    * - D-05: orIgnore insert into outbox only when refcount === 0
    */
-  async guardedUnpin(userId: string, cid: string): Promise<void> {
+  async guardedUnpin(
+    userId: string,
+    cid: string,
+    options?: { suppressCrossUserAudit?: boolean }
+  ): Promise<void> {
     let outboxRowInserted = false;
 
     await this.dataSource.transaction(async (manager) => {
       const pinnedCidRepo = manager.getRepository(PinnedCid);
       const pendingUnpinRepo = manager.getRepository(PendingUnpin);
 
-      // 1. Advisory xact lock — MUST be first statement (D-04)
+      // 1. Advisory xact lock — MUST be the first transactional statement (D-04)
+      // Compute the lock key inline so the lock is literally the first statement;
       // abs() avoids bigint-out-of-range on negative hashtext values (Pitfall 2)
-      const [{ h }] = (await manager.query(`SELECT abs(hashtext($1))::bigint AS h`, [cid])) as [
-        { h: string },
-      ];
-      await manager.query(`SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock($1)`, [h]);
+      await manager.query(`SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(abs(hashtext($1))::bigint)`, [cid]);
 
       // 2. Ownership check (D-01)
       const row = await pinnedCidRepo.findOne({ where: { userId, cid } });
       if (!row) {
         const otherRow = await pinnedCidRepo.findOne({ where: { cid } });
-        if (otherRow) {
+        if (otherRow && !options?.suppressCrossUserAudit) {
           this.logger.warn(`Cross-user unpin attempt userId=${userId} cid=${cid}`);
           this.metricsService.unpinCrossUserAttempts.inc();
         }
```

Merge complete and verified. Working tree clean, branch is **0 behind / 148 ahead** of `origin/main`.

## What happened

`origin/main` had independently merged **phase 42** (unpin guard #485) and **phase 43** (fuse write journal #487) via their own PR branches. This branch carried its *own* sequential implementation of phases 42, 43, **and** 44 — so phases 42/43 existed on both sides, implemented divergently. That produced **28 conflicts**.

## How I resolved them

The decisive fact: **none of the conflicted code files were touched by phase-44 work** — phase 44 (IPNS conflict handling) lives entirely in `packages/sdk-core`, `packages/sdk`, and `apps/web/src/hooks`, which didn't conflict. Every conflict was purely phase-42/43 duplication.

- **21 code files → took `origin/main`'s version** — its phase-42/43 implementations are the PR-reviewed canonical ones (e.g. main's `guardedUnpin` is a strict refinement of this branch's, adding `suppressCrossUserAudit`). Keeping two divergent copies of an already-merged feature would be wrong.
- **`PROJECT.md` + `STATE.md` → kept this branch's version** — it's the forward-most state (phase 44 done, "milestone complete"); main's #486 backlog/security content merged cleanly outside the conflict hunks.
- **5 phase-42/43 tracking docs → took `origin/main`'s** — canonical records matching the merged implementations.

## Verification

- **Compiles clean**: `pnpm typecheck` (crypto→core→api-client→sdk-core→sdk→web `tsc -b`) and `cargo check --workspace` both pass — confirms phase-44 code integrates with main's resolved phase-42/43 versions.
- No conflict markers remain; pre-commit lint-staged (eslint/prettier/markdownlint) passed; commit signed.

Merge commit: `592d449a1` (parents `969e26fc7` + `dcd1becb6`).

One thing worth flagging: this branch's effective unique contribution is **only phase 44** now — phases 42/43 came in from main. The branch is ready for whatever's next (PR, more phase-44 work, etc.).

96-- **Critical — local version history lost when the remote wins a file conflict.** `updateFileMetadata`'s 409 merge passed `remoteMeta.versions` (not `loser.versions`) as the second `mergeVersions` argument. When latest-wins selects the remote, the loser is the _local_ metadata, so the local writer's prior version history was silently dropped.
97-- **Why the audit missed it:** **T-44-09**'s "both directions tested" evidence over-trusted the remote-wins test, which used empty version arrays and never actually drove the remote-wins merge branch — `updateFileMetadata` stamps `updatedMetadata.modifiedAt = Date.now()`, so local wins unless the remote timestamp is future-dated. This is precisely the **T-44-21** "shallow conflict tests" class.

docs(phase-44): record post-audit CodeRabbit finding and fix in SECURITY.md

A CodeRabbit CLI review run after the SECURED verdict found a critical data-loss bug (remoteMeta.versions vs loser.versions in the file 409 merge) that T-44-09/T-44-21 over-trusted. Documents the gap and its fix (9f3c56181); threats_open stays 0.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Entire-Checkpoint: f3639d42e06b

----- 9f3c56181 -----
fix(sdk-core): preserve local version history when remote wins file conflict

The updateFileMetadata 409 conflict merge passed remoteMeta.versions as the second mergeVersions arg. When the remote write wins latest-wins, the loser is the LOCAL metadata, so this silently dropped the local writer's prior version history. Use loser.versions instead (identical in the local-wins case, correct in both directions).

Also re-export is409 from the sdk-core barrel (defined in errors.ts but not exported) and add unit tests for is409 plus a remote-wins history-preservation regression test. Found by CodeRabbit CLI review of phase 44.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
Entire-Checkpoint: d6e33b5f68ce

**CodeRabbit** — a CodeRabbit CLI review was run on the phase-44 diff *after* the `gsd-security-auditor` already returned a SECURED verdict, and it caught a **critical data-loss bug** the auditor missed:
- `updateFileMetadata`'s 409 merge passed `remoteMeta.versions` instead of `loser.versions` to `mergeVersions` → when the remote won a file conflict, the local writer's prior version history was silently dropped
- Fixed in `9f3c56181`, documented in `44-SECURITY.md` → "Post-Audit Findings (CodeRabbit CLI, 2026-06-14)"

So phase 44 went through the full gauntlet: `44-REVIEW.md` (gsd code-review), `44-SECURITY.md` (security-auditor + CodeRabbit re-review), and the `/simplify` pass.

One scoping note relevant to the merge we just did: those reviews covered the **phase-44 work** (this branch's unique contribution). The phase-42/43 code that just landed via the merge came from main's already-reviewed PRs (#485/#487), and since I resolved every 42/43 conflict by taking main's reviewed version wholesale, the merge introduced no new *unreviewed* code — just the mechanical resolution, which I verified compiles clean. I have all the information needed. The STATE.md Key Decisions are global/v1.1-level, not phase-44-specific, so I'll source phase-44 decisions from the summaries and verification artifacts. Here is the PR body draft.

---

### SUMMARY_PARAGRAPH
Phase 44 closes the IPNS lost-update class of bugs in `packages/sdk-core`. On a `409` conflict during republish, folder writes now re-resolve the authoritative remote metadata and run a three-way `mergeChildren` (children union + per-entry latest-wins reconcile) inside a bounded 4-attempt retry loop with exponential backoff and jitter, surfacing a typed `ConflictError` on exhaustion. File metadata gets equivalent protection via a CAS publish (`expectedSequenceNumber`) that closes the TOCTOU window and preserves the losing write's content as a recoverable `VersionEntry` under a `maxVersionsPerFile` cap. Every SDK and web call site (14 sites) was swept to pass pre-mutation `baseChildren` snapshots so the real three-way merge runs instead of the lossy union fallback, and to adopt the returned `publishedChildren` as the authoritative post-publish set. A full CRDT model was explicitly deferred to the CRDT-inbox research todo.

### CHANGES
- **44-01 ConflictError and mergeChildren** — Pure building blocks: typed `ConflictError` + three-way folder merge, permutation-matrix tested (TDD). Key files: `sdk-core/src/errors.ts`, `sdk-core/src/folder/merge.ts`, `folder-merge.test.ts`
- **44-02 Merge-and-Republish Retry Loop** — 4-attempt merge-and-republish loop in `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` with 409 three-way merge, backoff+jitter, exhaustion `ConflictError`. Key files: `sdk-core/src/folder/index.ts`, `folder.test.ts`
- **44-03 File CAS Publish and Conflict Merge** — File TOCTOU closed via CAS publish; loser preserved as recoverable `VersionEntry`, latest-wins semantics, `maxVersionsPerFile` cap. Key files: `sdk-core/src/file/index.ts`, `file.test.ts`
- **44-04 SDK Caller baseChildren Sweep** — 14 SDK sites (8 `client.ts`, 2 `bin`, 4 `shared-write`) pass pre-mutation `baseChildren`; shared-write paths are the D-08 headline fix. Key files: `sdk/src/client.ts`, `sdk/src/bin/index.ts`, `sdk/src/share/shared-write.ts`
- **44-05 Web Hook Call Sites Adoption** — Web hooks adopt new `updateFileMetadata` CAS contract + pass `baseChildren` at 3 folder re-publish sites; `ConflictError`-gated logging. Key files: `apps/web/src/hooks/useFileOperations.ts`, `useFileVersions.ts`
- **44-06 CR-01 publishedChildren adoption** — Surface merged `publishedChildren` from `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish`, adopt at all SDK + web store-write sites; closes one-write-later re-drop regression. Key files: `sdk-core/src/folder/index.ts`, `sdk/src/client.ts`, `apps/web/src/hooks/useSharedWriteOps.ts` (+11 more)
- **44-07 CR-02 prunedCids Reference Filter + WR-08** — Filter accumulated `prunedCids` against the merged record's referenced-CID set so a remote-resurrected CID is never unpinned. Key files: `sdk-core/src/file/index.ts`, `file.test.ts`

### REQUIREMENTS
No REQ-IDs tagged. All 7 plans trace to todo `2026-06-11-ipns-409-retry-lost-update` (audit gap closure). Internal tracking tags: D-08, CR-01, CR-02, WR-08.

### VERIFICATION
- Status: **passed** (previous `gaps_found`; both round-1 blockers CR-01/CR-02 now closed). sdk-core suite 196/196 green.
- Human verification required: **None** — all phase behaviors are unit-testable and verified by code inspection + the full vitest suite.
- Post-audit: CodeRabbit CLI review caught + fixed a critical version-history data-loss bug (commit `9f3c56181`).

### KEY_DECISIONS
- Folder 409 → re-resolve authoritative remote + three-way `mergeChildren` (children union, per-entry reconcile) before republish; bounded to 4 attempts with exp backoff + ±50% jitter.
- File metadata → CAS publish via `expectedSequenceNumber`; latest-wins by `modifiedAt`, loser content preserved as a `VersionEntry` (bounded to 2 attempts); overflow beyond `maxVersionsPerFile` → `prunedCids`.
- Always snapshot `baseChildren` as a shallow copy immediately before mutation (not after, not from `updatedChildren`) to enable the merge rather than the lossy union fallback.
- Return `publishedChildren` (the exact just-published set, no alias) and adopt it at all 14 store-write sites so refs and React state converge on the merged children (CR-01 convergence).
- Filter `prunedCids` against the merged record's referenced CIDs (`cid` + `versions[].cid`) before unpinning, preventing destruction of remote-resurrected content (CR-02).
- Full CRDT model deferred; server IPNS-seq self-increment divergence (AR-44-01/02) accepted as out-of-scope, mitigated operationally by re-resolve + re-sign per attempt.

---

import {
  uploadToSharedFolder,
  createSharedSubfolder,
  renameInSharedFolder,
  deleteFromSharedFolder,
  updateSharedFile,
  updateSharePermission,
  type SharedWriteContext,
} from '../share/shared-write';

import {
  updateFolderMetadataAndPublish,
  addToIpfs,
  batchPublishIpnsRecords,
  createAndPublishIpnsRecord,
} from '@cipherbox/sdk-core';
import { wrapKey, encryptAesGcm } from '@cipherbox/crypto';


=== updateSharedFile body (lines 434-530) ===
}): Promise<void> {
  // 1. Encrypt new content
  const fileKey = generateFileKey();
  const iv = generateIv();

  try {
    const ciphertext = await encryptAesGcm(params.newContent, fileKey, iv);

    // 2. Upload encrypted content to IPFS
    const { cid: contentCid } = await addToIpfs(params.ctx, new Uint8Array(ciphertext));

    // 3. Wrap fileKey with owner's public key
    const ownerWrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, params.ownerPublicKey);
    const fileKeyEncrypted = bytesToHex(ownerWrappedKey);

    // 4. Get the file's IPNS private key
    const ipnsPrivKey = await params.getFileIpnsKeyFn(params.filePointer.id);
    if (!ipnsPrivKey) {
      throw new Error('Cannot update: no file IPNS key available');
    }

    try {
      // 5. Resolve current file metadata
      const { metadata: currentMeta } = await resolveFileMetadata(
        params.filePointer.fileMetaIpnsName,
        params.folderKey,
        params.ctx
      );

      // 6. Update file metadata (publishes internally via CAS — Plan 03)
      await updateFileMetadata({
        fileIpnsPrivateKey: ipnsPrivKey,
        fileMetaIpnsName: params.filePointer.fileMetaIpnsName,
        folderKey: params.folderKey,
        currentMetadata: currentMeta,
        updates: {
          cid: contentCid,
          fileKeyEncrypted,
          fileIv: bytesToHex(iv),
          size: params.newContent.length,
          encryptionMode: 'GCM',
        },
        createVersion: false,
        ctx: params.ctx,
      });

      // Note: batchPublishIpnsRecords for the file record was here pre-Plan-03.
      // updateFileMetadata now publishes internally with CAS; the separate publish
      // has been removed to avoid double-publish. prunedCids from version overflow
      // are dropped here (pre-existing Phase-42 deferred leak — not regressed).

      // 8. Update share_key for recipient
      const recipientWrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, params.recipientPublicKey);
      try {
        await params.addShareKeysFn(params.shareId, [
          {
            keyType: 'file',
            itemId: params.filePointer.id,
            encryptedKey: bytesToHex(recipientWrappedKey),
          },
        ]);
      } catch (err) {
        console.warn('[shared-write] Failed to update share_key after file edit:', err);
      }
    } finally {
      ipnsPrivKey.fill(0);
    }
  } finally {
    fileKey.fill(0);
  }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Function 6: updateSharePermission
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * Update a share's permission level.
 *
 * Thin callback wrapper consistent with the existing revokeShare pattern.
 */
export async function updateSharePermission(params: {
  shareId: string;
  permission: string;
  encryptedIpnsKey?: string;
  updatePermissionFn: (
    shareId: string,
    body: { permission: string; encryptedIpnsKey?: string }
  ) => Promise<void>;
}): Promise<void> {
  await params.updatePermissionFn(params.shareId, {
    permission: params.permission,
    encryptedIpnsKey: params.encryptedIpnsKey,
  });
}
```

> AGENT

Root cause found — and it's a **stale test, not a code bug**. The `updateSharedFile` implementation comment (shared-write.ts:510) says it plainly:

> `updateFileMetadata` now publishes internally with CAS; the separate publish has been removed to avoid double-publish.

Phase-44 Plan-03 moved IPNS publishing *inside* `updateFileMetadata` (CAS), so `updateSharedFile` no longer calls `batchPublishIpnsRecords` directly. But the test (line 348) still asserts `batchPublishIpnsRecords` was called. The implementation is correct; the assertion is stale and was missed because phase-44 verification ran sdk-core (196/196) but not the full `packages/sdk` suite. The `updateFileMetadata` assertion on line 347 already covers "and publishes."

Fixing the test:

## Two failures, both fixed

**1. Test failure (`packages/sdk`)** — stale test, not a code bug.
- `updateSharedFile > ... and publishes` asserted `batchPublishIpnsRecords` was called, but phase-44 Plan-03 deliberately moved file-record publishing *inside* `updateFileMetadata` (CAS) to avoid double-publishing. The implementation comment said so explicitly.
- Removed the stale assertion (the `updateFileMetadata` assertion already covers "and publishes"). Verified locally: **14/14 pass**.
- Committed as `test(sdk): drop stale batchPublishIpnsRecords assertion…`.
- *Why it escaped phase-44 verification:* sdk-core was checked (196/196), but the full `packages/sdk` suite wasn't — CI was first to run it against the final state.

**2. Release Preview failure (required check)** — 2 malformed commit messages.
- `feat 44-02:` / `test 44-02:` (space instead of `(scope)`) touch `packages/sdk-core`; the script enforces conventional messages on commits touching versioned packages.
- Per your call, reworded via `filter-branch` → `feat(44-02):` / `test(44-02):` (+ the docs one). Verified the rewrite is **message-only**: the tree is byte-identical to before, the `origin/main` merge is preserved, and the only remaining non-conventional subject is the merge commit (which the script skips).

=================== comment 3409841564 ===================
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**`fetchQuota` rejection expectation does not match the service behavior.**

This test expects `logger.warn('quota reconcile failed', fetchError)` after a rejected `fetchQuota`, but `deleteFile` currently only warns on a resolved falsy result (`then(ok => !ok)`), not on rejection. So this assertion is inconsistent with the implementation contract and will fail.

<details>
<summary>Suggested test fix</summary>

```diff
-  const fetchError = new Error('quota endpoint unreachable');
-  mockFetchQuota.mockRejectedValueOnce(fetchError);
+  mockFetchQuota.mockResolvedValueOnce(false);

   await expect(deleteFile('bafytest456', 2048)).resolves.toBeUndefined();

   // Give the fire-and-forget microtask a chance to settle
   await Promise.resolve();

-  expect(logger.warn).toHaveBeenCalledWith('quota reconcile failed', fetchError);
+  expect(logger.warn).toHaveBeenCalledWith('quota reconcile failed');
```
</details>

As per coding guidelines, `**/*.test.ts`: “Focus on test coverage, edge cases, and test quality. Ensure tests are meaningful and not just for coverage metrics.”

> AGENT

Finding 1 **confirmed valid**. The implementation (`delete.service.ts:24-25`) is:
```js
void quotaStore.fetchQuota().then((ok) => {
  if (!ok) logger.warn('quota reconcile failed');   // one arg, only on resolved-falsy; no .catch
});
```
The test mocks a **rejection** and expects `logger.warn('quota reconcile failed', fetchError)` (two args) — both wrong: there's no `.catch`, so a rejection never warns, and the resolved-falsy warn passes only one arg. The test would fail if it ran — but it's a `.spec.ts`, so web vitest silently skips it (the known repo gotcha). CodeRabbit's fix aligns it with the contract.

## Review resolution complete — PR #488

**2 / 2 CodeRabbit threads resolved**, both valid, both fixed in `a9f14eeaa`.

| Thread | Finding | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| `delete.service.spec.ts:65` (🟠 Major) | Test mocks a `fetchQuota` rejection + expects 2-arg `logger.warn`, but impl only warns on resolved-falsy with 1 arg | **Deleted the file** — it's a stale branch-phase-42 duplicate of the canonical `delete.service.test.ts` (from #485), which already has the correct test and actually runs. The `.spec.ts` never ran (web vitest = `*.test.ts` only). Zero coverage lost. |
| `file.test.ts:158` (🟡 Minor) | Shared `mockPrivateKey` is zeroized in-place by `updateFileMetadata` (`file/index.ts:399`) → all-zero key after test 1 | **`let mockPrivateKey` + reinit in `beforeEach`** so each case gets a fresh non-zero key |

**Validation:** `file.test.ts` 15/15, `delete.service.test.ts` 3/3 (targeted runs). New CI run: **no failures**.

**Root-cause note:** the `delete.service.spec.ts` finding was the same merge-artifact class as the phase-42/43 conflicts — the branch's earlier duplicate surviving alongside main's canonical version (here it slipped the merge because the filenames differ, so it wasn't a conflict). CodeRabbit suggested patching the assertion; deleting the dead duplicate was the better fix. I scanned for others — this was the only one.

Both threads have explanatory replies posted and are marked resolved.
